## Features & Traits

### Monk Features

**Core Monk Traits (PHB-2024 101)**

**Martial Arts (PHB-2024 101)**
You gain the following benefits when unarmed or wielding only Monk weapons, provided you aren't wearing armor or wielding a Shield.
- You can make an Unarmed Strike as a Bonus Action.
- You can roll 1d8 in place of the normal damage from your Unarmed Strikes or Monk weapons.
- You can use Dex. instead of Str. for the attack and damage rolls of your Unarmed Strikes and Monk Weapons. In addition, when you use the Grapple or Shove option of your Unarmed Strike, you can use Dex. instead of Str. to determine the save DC.
- Unarmed Strike: 1 Bonus Action

**Unarmored Defense (PHB-2024 101)**
While you aren't wearing armor or wielding a Shield, your base AC equals 16.

**Monk's Focus (PHB-2024 101)**
Your focus and martial training allows you to harness a well of energy within yourself called Focus Points. You have 8 Focus Points and regain all expended points after you finish a Short or Long Rest. You can expend these points to enhance or fuel certain Monk features. You start knowing three such features:
- Flurry of Blows. You can expend 1 Focus Point to make two Unarmed Strikes as a Bonus Action.
- Patient Defense. You can take the Disengage action as a Bonus Action or expend 1 Focus Point to take both the Disengage and Dodge actions as a Bonus Action.
- Step of the Wind. You can take the Dash action as a Bonus Action or expend 1 Focus Point to take both the Disengage and Dash actions as a Bonus Action, and your jump distance is doubled for the turn.

Features that use Focus Points may require your target to make a saving throw. The save DC equals 8 + Wis. modifier + Prof. Bonus.

| Focus Points: 8 / Short Rest • Special |
| Flurry of Blows: 1 Bonus Action |
| Patient Defense: 1 Bonus Action |
| Step of the Wind: 1 Bonus Action |

**Unarmored Movement (PHB-2024 102)**
Your speed increases by 15 ft. while you aren't wearing armor or wielding a Shield.

**Uncanny Metabolism (PHB-2024 102)**
Once per Long Rest, when you roll Initiative you can regain all expended Focus Points, and regain 1d8+8 HP.
| 1 / Long Rest • Special |

**Deflect Attacks (PHB-2024 102)**
When an attack roll that includes Bludgeoning, Piercing, or Slashing damage hits you, you can take a Reaction to reduce the attack's total damage by 1d10+12. If you reduce the damage to 0, you can expend 1 Focus Point to redirect some of the attack's force. If you do, choose a creature you can see within 5 ft. of you if it was melee, or a creature you can see within 60 ft. that isn't behind Total Cover if the attack was ranged. The creature must succeed on a DC 13 Dex. saving throw or take 2d8+4 damage of the same type dealt by the attack.
| Deflect Attack: 1 Reaction |
| Deflect Attack: Redirect Attack: 1 Reaction |

**Monk Subclass (PHB-2024 103) — Warrior of Shadow**

**Shadow Arts (PHB-2024 105)**
You've learned to draw on the power from the Shadowfell and gain the following benefits:
- You can expend 1 Focus Point to cast Darkness without spell components, and when you do, you can see within the spell's area. While the spell persists, you can move its area of Darkness to a space within 60 ft. of you at the start of each of your turns.
- You gain Darkvision with a range of 60 ft., and if you already have Darkvision, its range is increased by 60 ft.
- You know the Minor Illusion spell, and Wisdom is your spellcasting ability for it.

**Ability Score Improvement (PHB-2024 103)**

**Slow Fall (PHB-2024 103)**
You can take a Reaction when you fall to reduce any falling damage by 40.
| 1 Reaction |

**Extra Attack (PHB-2024 103)**
You can attack twice instead of once when you take the Attack action.

**Stunning Strike (PHB-2024 103)**
Once per turn, when you hit with a Monk weapon or Unarmed Strike, you can expend 1 Focus Point to attempt a stunning strike. The target must make a DC 13 Con. saving throw. On a failed save, the target has the Stunned condition until the start of your next turn. On a successful save, the target's Speed is halved until the start of your next turn, and the next attack roll made against it has Advantage.
| 1 Action |

**Empowered Strikes (PHB-2024 103)**
Whenever you deal damage with your Unarmed Strike, it can deal your choice of Force damage or its normal damage type.
| Special |

**Shadow Step (PHB-2024 105)**
While entirely within Dim Light or Darkness, you can use a Bonus Action to teleport up to 60 ft. to an unoccupied space that you can see that's also in Dim Light or Darkness. You have Advantage on the next melee attack you make before the end of the current turn.
| 1 Bonus Action |

**Evasion (PHB-2024 103)**
When subjected to an effect that allows you to make a Dex. saving throw to take only half damage, you instead take no damage if you succeed, and only half damage if you fail. You don't benefit from this feature if you have the Incapacitated condition.

**8: Ability Score Improvement (PHB-2024 103)**

## Feats

**Grappler (PHB-2024 204)**
Ability Score Increase. Increase your Str. or Dex. by 1.

Punch and Grab. On your turn, when you hit a creature with an Unarmed Strike you can use both the Damage and the Grapple option. You can use this benefit only once per turn.

Attack Advantage. You have Advantage on attack rolls against a creature Grappled by you.

Fast Wrestler. You don't have to spend extra movement to move a creature Grappled by you if the creature is your size or smaller.

**Street Justice (FRHoF 41)**
You gain the following benefits.
- Ability Score Increase. +1 Str. or Dex., to a maximum of 20.
- Headlock. Allies have Advantage on attacks against a creature Grappled by you.
- Sturdy Knot. When you use Chain, Manacles, or Rope to bind a creature, add 3 to the DC to escape or burst through.
- Tough Talk. A creature's Hostile attitude doesn't impose Disadvantage on your Charisma (Intimidation) checks to influence that creature.

**Sailor Ability Score Improvements (PHB-2024 183)**
| Increase two scores (+2 / +1) |

**Tavern Brawler (PHB-2024 202)**
Enhanced Unarmed Strike. When you hit with your Unarmed Strike and deal damage, you can deal 1d4-1 Bludgeoning damage instead of the normal damage of an Unarmed Strike.

Damage Rerolls. Whenever you roll a damage die for your Unarmed Strike, you can reroll the die if it rolls a 1, and you must use the new roll.

Improvised Weaponry. You have proficiency with improvised weapons.

Push. When you hit a creature with an Unarmed Strike, you can deal damage to the target and also push it 5 ft. away from you. You can use this benefit only once per turn.
| Enhanced Unarmed Strike: 1 Action |
| Push: Special |

## Personality

### Character Backstory
Born in a bustling coastal city known for its vibrant docks and seafaring trade, Zalthir "Zal" Thunderscale grew up amidst the salty air and the roar of the ocean. As a Brass Dragonborn, he bore the distinctive golden-hued scales of his kind, glimmering like the sun on the water's surface. His early life was marked by the stories of his ancestors—noble protectors of the skies who championed freedom and adventure.

Zalthir's parents were skilled sailors, known for their expertise in navigating treacherous waters and trading with distant lands. They instilled in him a love for the sea, teaching him the ways of sailing, survival, and respect for the natural world. From a young age, Zal felt a calling toward the ocean, often spending hours by the docks, dreaming of the adventures that awaited him beyond the horizon.

However, it was not just the thrill of sailing that captivated him. Zalthir's upbringing was also steeped in the teachings of a local monastery, where a sect of monks revered the elements, including the mighty ocean. Drawn to their philosophy of harmony and discipline, he began training under their guidance, learning martial arts and the importance of balance, both in body and mind. His instructors recognized his innate connection to the elemental forces, particularly the gentle but powerful currents of the sea.

As he matured, Zalthir embraced his dual passions: the life of a sailor and the path of a monk. He joined a merchant crew, where he quickly became known for his agility and fierce dedication. The ship became his second home, and the crew, his family. They sailed to distant ports, trading goods and sharing tales of adventure. But the life of a sailor was not without its dangers. Zal faced storms, sea monsters, and rival pirates, all while honing his skills in unarmed combat and meditation during the quiet nights at sea.

<!-- Backstory: zalthir_backstory.md -->

Zalthir’s backstory is steeped in the rigid, unforgiving discipline of a highly secretive monastic order, shaped by pragmatic survivalism and shadowed by the mysterious circumstances of his draconic lineage. Through the agentic flows of his tactical decisions and internal reflections, we can infer a comprehensive profile of the Shadow Shepherd's origins.
The Monastic Crucible Zalthir was raised and trained in a monastery that valued absolute silence, discretion, and the avoidance of frontal conflict
. This training was not merely physical but deeply psychological and metaphysical. He was taught to solve complex spatial and temporal anomalies, such as understanding how magical pressure can cause time to "snag" and how a Shadow Monk must create a void for reality to safely pass through
. His education also extended to esoteric survival skills; he studied under an ancient monk known as "The Garden Shadow," who taught him the precise, delicate art of cutting dangerous flora like lichen and tending to deadly gardens
.
The Doctrine of Silence and Efficiency The monastery instilled in Zalthir a profound intolerance for noise and emotional outbursts. He frequently reflects on how, in his order, children who could not maintain silence were isolated in a room until they understood the necessity of quiet
. He views the chaotic, overlapping conversations of his current allies as a tactical liability and openly laments that his masters taught him "how to make people blind, and not how to make them silent"
. This extreme discipline strips away sentimentality; he does not view brutal interrogation as torture, but rather as practical "motivation"
, and he approaches combat with cold, methodical dismantling of the most dangerous targets first
.
The Legacy of Korrh Erann Zalthir’s survival strategy in Underdark is directly modeled after the teachings of Korrh Erann, a legendary monk from his order who was once trapped in the subterranean depths and pursued by Drow
. Korrh Erann taught that engaging Drow scouts was a dangerous gamble and that relentless flight was the only path to survival
. Zalthir has internalized this historical lesson, consistently advocating for evasion over unnecessary combat to preserve the party's resources
.
The Secret of His Draconic Blood Zalthir is a brass dragonborn, a fact that occasionally confuses Underdark denizens who expect chromatic red dragons due to the local influence of Themberchaud
. However, his heritage conceals a darker secret. During his battle with the maddened Medusa, Neheedra Duskryn, the creature used her venomous taunts to mock his "draconic heritage and the circumstances of his birth"
. This indicates that his lineage carries a stigma or hidden shame—likely tying his brass ancestry to chromatic or abyssal corruption—which fuels his stoic detachment and desire to control his own destiny.

### Zalthir

**Current Status:** Alive  
**Current Location:** Deneir’s Sanctum, under guard  
**Allegiance/Faction:** Former the Ember Vanguard; now an independent investigator and de facto leader of the group; affiliated with Candlekeep as an The Avowed scholar assigned to the Jimjar thread; bound to Glabbagool as a sentient companion  

**Current Possessions / Notable Items:**  
- Eldritch Claw Tattoo (active, granting 15-foot reach via tentacles)  
- The Stone Guard passes (from Gracklstugh)  
- Drow spider-silk cloak (taken from Asha Vandree Vandree)  
- Potion of Flying (stolen from Alkrist’s safe, concealed beneath skin via Glabbagool)  
- Glabbagool (gelatinous cube bonded to his left forearm; functions as an extension of his body, capable of acid secretion, squeezing through narrow spaces, and sensing environmental anomalies)  
- Minor Illusion spellbook (used for tactical deception)  
- Collection of fungi (including Ormu and Nilhogg's Nose’s nose mushrooms)  
- List of names (retrieved from earlier captivity)  
- Ice mirror (used to deflect Medusa’s gaze)  

**Notable Spells / Feats / Abilities:**  
- **Darkness** (cast with surgical precision; used to control battlefield geometry, conceal allies, and disrupt vision)  
- **Shadow Step** (teleports through shadows; used for repositioning and surprise attacks)  
- **Stunning Strike** (monastic ability used to immobilize enemies)  
- **Eldritch Maul** (transformed hands into 15-foot tentacles; used to grapple and control targets)  
- **Minor Illusion** (used to fabricate structures and deceive targets, e.g., posing as an architect)  
- **Street Justice** (feat formalizing grappling and binding mechanics; enables two-turn setup to pin enemies for Daz’s Cloud of Daggers)  
- **Shadow Monk Training** (from The Garden Shadow; includes precise lichen harvesting, redirection of magical energy through shadow space, and silent movement)  
- **Monastic Discipline** (emphasizes discretion, avoidance of frontal conflict, emotional detachment, and self-administered discipline under fear or trauma)  

**Current Assignment / Role and Key Relationships:**  
- **Role:** Primary investigator, tactical strategist, and moral anchor of the group. Acts as the group’s silent architect, analyzing patterns, exploiting weaknesses, and executing high-precision maneuvers.  
- **Reports to:** None; operates independently but aligns with Daz’s leadership in tactical matters.  
- **Key Relationships:**  
  - **Glabbagool:** Bonded companion; Zalthir considers him a student of monastic discipline and an extension of his own tactical capacity.  
  - **Daz:** Tactical partner; Zalthir has developed the “Ember Grapple” synergy with Daz’s area-of-effect spells.  
  - **Gyrgum:** Mutual respect; Zalthir recognizes Gyrgum’s insight and moral clarity, silently approving his decisions.  
  - **Kalan Strongbranch:** Under investigation; Zalthir suspects Kalan Strongbranch is acting out of self-preservation, not principle.  
  - **Jimjar:** Subject of ongoing investigation; Zalthir confirmed Jimjar’s divine-level immunities and resistances, recognizing him as non-human.  
  - **Bookwyrm (First Reader):** Officially under her authority at Candlekeep; Zalthir is skeptical of her impartiality.  
  - **Asha Vandree Vandree:** Former adversary; Zalthir killed her decisively and took her cloak, viewing her as a symbol of structural erasure.  

**Defining Recent Actions:**  
- Successfully stole the Potion of Flying from Alkrist’s safe during the chaos of the Sanctum confrontation, concealing it beneath his skin via Glabbagool.  
- Grappled and neutralized a helmed horror using Glabbagool’s acid to dissolve its armor, deducing the constructs were tracking the key’s magical resonance, not its bearer.  
- Analyzed and exposed Kalan Strongbranch’s flawed logic regarding the Zone of Truth spell, questioning the morality of his academic principles over justice.  
- Identified Sylvira Savikas’s appearances as spanning a two-hour window, proving the use of multiple or complex magical effects beyond standard polymorph.  
- Confirmed the murder of Janussi was a double-strike: poisoning via “Midnight Tears” concealed in a book, followed by heart removal to prevent resurrection, with Daral Yashenti framed.  
- Accepted the frightened condition during the fight with Ilvara Mizzrym Mizzrym as self-administered monastic discipline, refusing to exploit angles or resist the rule — a defining moment of internal conviction.  
- Killed Asha Vandree Vandree with the Eldritch Claw Tattoo, acting when others hesitated, and refused to justify the act — “I made it. That’s all.”  
- Offered Glabbagool monastic training, recognizing his instinct to reach toward problems as a higher form of discipline than Zalthir himself mastered.  

**Revealed Motivations or Secrets:**  
- Zalthir’s monastic training emphasized control through proximity, silence, and discipline — not violence. He believes the true purpose of the monastery was learning to hold something without letting go, even when it humiliates or incapacitates you.  
- He distrusts convenient explanations, but does not dismiss them — he believes the world is built on debts, hidden motives, and structural silences.  
- He is haunted by the erasure he experienced as a Drow — treated as furniture — and sees it mirrored in Candlekeep’s culture of performative civility and hidden grudges.  
- He has been maintaining a secret column since Kaelira Duskryn revealed someone powerful was paying to protect a man he couldn’t understand — the patron entry has remained blank for months.  
- He believes the most dangerous person in any room is the one who has already decided they’ve won.  
- He is no longer seeking escape or freedom — he is seeking to understand the architecture of power, deception, and silence that governs the world.  

## Uncertainty  
- Contradictory dragonborn lineage: Some facts label Zalthir as a bronze dragonborn, others as gold — later fact (ch28) confirms he is bronze, and Duergar mistakenly perceive him as red due to magical misperception.  
- Unclear origin of the “the Ember Vanguard” identity: Zalthir is repeatedly mistaken for an the Ember Vanguard member due to racial prejudice and magical distortion, but it is never confirmed whether he ever formally belonged to the group.  
- Ambiguous timeline of Glabbagool’s bonding: While Glabbagool is confirmed as bonded to Zalthir’s arm (ch58, ch61), the exact moment and mechanics of the bond are not described — only its effects.  
- Unclear attribution of “The Garden Shadow” teachings: Multiple instructors (Brother Heslin, Brother Quellin, Brother Brother Heslin) are cited as sources of Zalthir’s wisdom, but he consistently admits he does not remember who taught him what — suggesting unreliable memory or deliberate obfuscation.  
- Uncertain whether Zalthir is still physically carrying the list of names from ch01 — no later mention confirms its current location or status.  
- Unclear if Zalthir’s vow of silence (from ch03) remains active — he speaks frequently in later chapters, suggesting it was temporary or abandoned.  
- Unclear whether Zalthir’s “maybe list” for killing Daz is still active or merely a theoretical construct — no later confirmation or revision is given.  
- Uncertain if Zalthir’s perception of the Overbright as “violence” is a permanent psychological shift or situational trauma — no follow-up explores its long-term impact.  
- Unclear if Zalthir’s internal monologue about “Leemooggoogoon, Demogorgon, two-headed baby dragons” (ch23) reflects a belief, a joke, or a mental breakdown — no resolution is provided.  
- Ambiguous status of the “Jimjar thread” at Candlekeep: Zalthir is assigned to investigate it, but it is never confirmed whether he has completed it or what he discovered.

Zalthir has no formal arc score mechanic. This is a deliberate design choice — do not invent an arc score for this character and do not suggest creating one.

## Features & Traits

### Fighter Features

**Core Fighter Traits (PHB-2024 91)**

**Fighting Style (PHB-2024 91)**
You gain a Fighting Style feat of your choice, and whenever you gain a Fighter level, you can replace the feat you chose with a different Fighting Style feat.

**Second Wind (PHB-2024 91)**
As a Bonus Action, you can draw upon a limited well of physical and mental stamina and regain 1d10+8 HP. You can use this 3 times per Long Rest, and can regain one expended use when you finish a Short Rest. *3 / Long Rest • 1 Bonus Action*

**Weapon Mastery (PHB-2024 91)**

**Action Surge (PHB-2024 91)**
On your turn you can take one additional action, except the Magic action. You can use this feature 1 time(s) until you finish a Short or Long Rest. *1 / Short Rest • Special*

**Tactical Mind (PHB-2024 91)**
When you fail an ability check, you can expend a use of Second Wind, and instead of regaining HP, you roll 1d10 and add the result to the ability check. If the check still fails, this use of Second Wind isn't expended. *Special*

### Battle Master (Fighter Subclass, PHB-2024 92)

**Combat Superiority (PHB-2024 93)**
You learn maneuvers that are fueled by special dice called Superiority Dice. Many maneuvers enhance an attack in some way. You can use only one maneuver per attack. You have 5d8 Superiority Dice, which are expended when used. You regain any expended Superiority Die when you finish a Short or Long Rest. *Superiority Dice: 5 / Short Rest • 1 Action*

**Student of War (PHB-2024 94)**
You gain proficiency with one type of Artisan's Tools of your choice, and in one skill of your choice from the skills available to Fighters at level 1.

**Maneuver Options (PHB-2024 94)**
You know 5 maneuvers of your choice - many of which enhance an attack in some way. You can use only one maneuver per attack. If a maneuver requires a saving throw, the DC equals 8 + Strength/Dexterity modifier (your choice) + Proficiency Bonus.

**Commanding Presence**
When you make a Cha. (Intimidation, Performance, or Persuasion) check, you can expend one Superiority Die and add that die to the roll. *Maneuver: Commanding Presence: Special*

**Feinting Attack**
As a Bonus Action, you can expend one Superiority Die to feint, targeting one creature within 5 ft. of you. Your next attack roll against the target this turn has Advantage, and if it hits, you add the Superiority Die to the damage roll. *Maneuver: Feinting Attack: 1 Bonus Action*

**Menacing Attack**
When you hit a creature with an attack roll, you can expend one Superiority Die and add the die to the attack's damage roll. The target must succeed on a Wis. saving throw, or have the Frightened condition until the end of your next turn. *Maneuver: Menacing Attack (Str.): 1 Action / Maneuver: Menacing Attack (Dex.): 1 Action*

**Precision Attack**
When you miss with an attack roll, you can expend one Superiority Die and add the die to the attack roll. *Maneuver: Precision Attack: 1 Action*

**Riposte**
When a creature misses you with a melee attack roll, you can take a Reaction to expend one Superiority die to make a melee attack roll with a weapon or Unarmed Strike against that creature. If you hit, add the Superiority die to the attack's damage. *Maneuver: Riposte: 1 Reaction*

**4: Weapon Mastery (PHB-2024 91)**

**Ability Score Improvement (PHB-2024 92)**

**Extra Attack (PHB-2024 92)**
You can attack 2 times instead of once whenever you take the Attack action on your turn.

**Tactical Shift (PHB-2024 92)**
Whenever you activate your Second Wind with a Bonus Action, you can move up to 15 ft. without provoking Opportunity Attacks. *Second Wind: Tactical Shift: 1 Bonus Action*

**Know Your Enemy (PHB-2024 94)**
As a Bonus Action, you can discern the Immunities, Resistances, and Vulnerabilities of a creature you can see within 30 ft. of you. You can use this once per Long Rest, and can restore a use of it by expending one Superiority Die (no action required). *1 / Long Rest • 1 Bonus Action*

**6: Ability Score Improvement (PHB-2024 92)**

**8: Ability Score Improvement (PHB-2024 92)**

### Dwarf Species Traits

**Creature Type (PHB-2024 188)**
You are a Humanoid.

**Size (PHB-2024 188)**
You are Medium.

**Speed (PHB-2024 188)**
Your speed is 30 ft.

**Darkvision (PHB-2024 188)**
You have Darkvision with a range of 120 ft.

**Dwarven Resilience (PHB-2024 188)**
You have Resistance to Poison damage. You also have Advantage on saving throws you make to avoid or end the Poisoned condition.

**Dwarven Toughness (PHB-2024 188)**
Your HP maximum increases by 8.

**Stonecunning (PHB-2024 188)**
As a Bonus Action, you gain Tremorsense with a range of 60 ft. for 10 minutes. You must be on or touching a natural or worked stone surface to use this Tremorsense. You can use this Bonus Action 3 times per Long Rest. *Stonecunning (Tremorsense): 3 / Long Rest • 1 Bonus Action*

### Weapon Mastery Properties

**Dart (Vex)**
Vex. If you hit a creature with a Dart and deal damage to it, you have Advantage on your next attack roll against that creature before the end of your next turn.

**Hand Crossbow (Vex)**
Vex. If you hit a creature with a Hand Crossbow and deal damage to it, you have Advantage on your next attack roll against that creature before the end of your next turn. *Vex (Hand Crossbow): 1 Action*

**Javelin (Slow)**
Slow. If you hit a creature with a Javelin and deal damage to the creature, you can reduce its Speed by 10 ft. until the start of your next turn. If the creature is hit more than once with this property, the Speed reduction doesn't exceed 10 ft. *Slow (Javelin): 1 Action*

**Longsword (Sap)**
Sap. If you hit a creature with a Longsword, that creature has Disadvantage on its next attack roll before the start of your next turn. *Sap (Longsword): 1 Action*

*Vex (Dart): 1 Action*

## Feats

**Blind Fighting (PHB-2024 209)**
You have Blindsight with a range of 10 ft.

**Sentinel (PHB-2024 207)**
Ability Score Increase. Increase your Str. or Dex. by 1. Guardian. Immediately after a creature within 5 ft. of you takes the Disengage action or hits a target other than you with an attack, you can make an Opportunity Attack against that creature. Halt. When you hit a creature with an Opportunity Attack, the creature's Speed becomes 0 for the rest of the current turn.

**Lucky (PHB-2024 201)**
Luck Points. You have 3 Luck Points that you can spend on the benefits below. You regain expended Luck Points after a Long Rest. Advantage. When you roll a d20 for a D20 Test, you can spend 1 Luck Point to give yourself Advantage on the roll. Disadvantage. When a creature rolls a d20 for an attack roll against you, you can spend 1 Luck Point to impose Disadvantage on that roll. *Luck Points: 3 / Long Rest • Special*

**Alert (PHB-2024 200)**
Initiative Proficiency. When you roll Initiative, add +3 to the roll. Initiative Swap. Immediately after you roll Initiative, you can swap your Initiative with the Initiative of one willing ally in the same combat. You can't make this swap if you or the ally has the Incapacitated condition.

**Strike of the Giants (Hill Strike) (GotG 19)**
Once per turn, when you hit a target with a melee weapon attack or a ranged weapon attack using a thrown weapon, you can imbue the attack with an additional effect: Hill Strike. The target takes an extra 1d6 damage of the weapon's type. If the target is a creature, it must succeed on a Strength saving throw (DC 15) or have the prone condition. You can use this feat 3 times per long rest. *Strike of the Giants: Hill Strike: 3 / Long Rest • Special*

**Ability Score Increases (PHB-2024)**
When determining your character's ability scores, increase one score by 2 and a different one by 1, or increase three scores by 1. *Increase two scores (+2 / +1). Increase one score by 2 and a different score by 1.*

## Personality
- **Traits:** Every avalanche begins as a single pebble.

<!-- Backstory: thorin_backstory.md -->

Thorin’s backstory is an anomaly within traditional dwarven lore, shaped entirely by the turbulent events of the surface world during the recent War of the Giants
. By synthesizing the partial revelations from your session logs, we can infer the following profile of his origins and psychological framework:
The Hill Giant Upbringing Thorin was raised not in a dwarven stronghold, but by a tribe of hill giants
. This upbringing fundamentally altered his cultural markers; he speaks Dwarvish with a thick, unmistakable Giant accent
. Thorin considers this tribe his family and fondly recalls their direct, uncomplicated way of life, which was blessedly free from the convoluted plots and betrayals that characterize Underdark societies like those of the Drow and Duergar
.
Martial Training and Draconic Animus His martial prowess is heavily influenced by his giant kin. In combat, he employs specific, brutal tactics, such as the "hill strike," which he learned directly from a hill giant battle master
. Furthermore, Thorin has internalized the ancient, cosmic feud between giants and dragons. Because he lived among giants, he adopted their peculiar and intense antipathy toward dragonkind, a prejudice that severely triggered his temper when the party was forced into the service of the red dragon Themberchaud in Gracklstugh
.
Friction with Traditional Dwarves His unique history places him at severe odds with traditional shield dwarves like Eldeth Feldrun Feldrun
. Following the devastation of the War of the Giants, surface dwarves view all giants as relentless enemies of the "little folk"
. Thorin adamantly defends his adoptive family to these skeptics, insisting that his tribe consisted of "different giants" and using himself as living proof that peace between giants and smaller races is possible
.
Ideology of Radical Transparency Because of his straightforward giant upbringing, Thorin despises the secrecy, Non-Disclosure Agreements, and political maneuvering of Underdark
. He advocates for "radical transparency and honesty in relations," openly rejecting complex faction loyalties in favor of direct action
. When overwhelmed by the competing agendas of The Keepers of the Flame, The Stone Guard, and Gray Ghosts, his immediate instinct was a desire to simply "be my faction"
.

<!-- Ensemble dossier: npc_thorin.md -->

---
name: Thorin
type: npc
n_facts: 584
chapters: 1-61
---

### Thorin

**Current Status:** Alive  
**Current Location:** Surface, near Candlekeep or en route south (last seen preparing to use a reward to obtain a magical book to prove Bookwyrm (First Reader)’s guilt; last physical setting: Deneir’s Sanctum, following ambush of helmed horrors)  
**Allegiance/Faction:** Independent; self-identified as “the Ember Vanguard” (coined the name); no longer serving any faction, though formerly entangled with The Stone Guard, Duergar authorities, and The Zhentarim-aligned pragmatism  
**Current Possessions / Notable Items:**  
- **Dawnbringer** — sentient, radiant sunblade; referred to as “she,” with whom Thorin has a deep, negotiated bond; used for combat, healing, and philosophical dialogue; recently carried in a Vaultmaster Locking Scabbard as a diplomatic tool  
- **Recovered tooth** — taken as a trophy by Ilvara Mizzrym at Velkynvelve; reclaimed after her defeat; held as a sentimental object  
- **Necromantic pearl** — recovered from the heart of Ilvara Mizzrym’s corrupted fungal ritual site; considered valuable and dangerous; kept in pocket  
- **Diggermattock Miners’ patch** — magically mended onto his gear, earned for identifying the psychic center in the Hall of Melting Mirrors  
- **Light crossbow** — used temporarily in combat; not his preferred weapon  
- **Darts** — primary ranged weapon; he has acquired a Sharpshooter’s instinct for thrown weapons  
- **Vaultmaster Locking Scabbard** — used to negotiate with Dawnbringer; a tool of diplomacy and control  

**Current Spells / Feats / Abilities:**  
- **Hill Strike** — battle technique learned from a hill giant battle master  
- **Sharpshooter’s instinct** — acquired proficiency with thrown weapons (javelins, darts); prefers reach and positioning  
- **Tremor Sense** — feat chosen for Underdark travel; now considered useless on surface terrain; source of frustration  
- **Healing ability** — demonstrated self-healing during combat (ch26)  
- **Combat pragmatism** — excels in direct melee, battlefield control, opportunity attacks, and exploiting terrain  
- **Contract negotiation** — trained by giants; understands legal language, seals, and enforceability  

**Current Assignment / Role and Key Relationships:**  
- **De facto leader** of the Ember Vanguard — orchestrates strategy, demands clarity, and pushes for tangible outcomes  
- **Primary wielder and companion of Dawnbringer** — treats the blade as a sentient friend, not a weapon; seeks to restore her connection to light; considers her gender “she”  
- **Trusted by group** — despite his blunt, chaotic style, he is relied upon for physical strength, tactical aggression, and moral grounding  
- **Last seen with:** Zalthir, Gyrgum, Daz, Glabbagool, Eldeth Feldrun (last interaction: Eldeth Feldrun promised clan embrace, but Thorin accepted her departure)  
- **Key relationship:** With **Kalan Strongbranch** — he chose to hand over the Candlekeep case to him to preserve his dignity after being fired; sees this as terrain-based justice  
- **Relationship with Dawnbringer:** Central to his identity; they have developed a ritual of negotiation (“Do I need to show you the scabbard again?”); he believes she needs to remember daylight and grieve  

**Defining Recent Actions (Collapsed):**  
- Led the ambush on helmed horrors in Deneir’s Sanctum, using Dawnbringer to land a “sap” effect and stabilize the party  
- Orchestrated the plan to expose Bookwyrm (First Reader) by using the investigation reward to obtain a magical book that retroactively proves her guilt — a calculated bureaucratic grift  
- Reclaimed his stolen tooth and the necromantic pearl from Ilvara Mizzrym’s ritual site, marking his transition from captive to agent of agency  
- Declined to kill Asha Vandree Vandree, recognizing her as a Lolth cleric requiring lethal neutralization — later corrected by Drow mercenaries  
- Chose not to intervene as Jorlan Duskryn confronted Ilvara Mizzrym, prioritizing emotional distance over tactical control  
- Destroyed the Heart Fungus in one round with Dawnbringer, accepting the moral weight of the act — “I felt a little bad, in the way you feel bad about something you’d do exactly the same way again”  
- Negotiated the group’s escape from Gracklstugh by advocating to ride Themberchaud — a recurring, absurdly pragmatic solution  
- Advocated for radical transparency, but chooses silence when speech adds nothing to the next ten seconds  
- Accepted his identity as a dwarf raised by hill giants, seeking integration with dwarven roots — now desires to be “an integrated person”  

**Revealed Motivations or Secrets:**  
- **Core drive:** To walk up to the problem and hit it with Dawnbringer until it stops — a philosophy forged in Underdark and hardened by trauma  
- **Hidden trauma:** Survived the War of the Giants when the ordning fell apart; carries the lessons of giants — direct, brutal, unyielding — even as he rejects their moral ambiguity  
- **Moral evolution:** From hopeful adventurer to hardened pragmatist who understands Underdark doesn’t care who you want to be; now embraces The Zhentarim Tactics — “willing to do what others won’t” — as survival, not ideology  
- **Secret:** His tooth was taken as a symbol of ownership by Ilvara Mizzrym; reclaiming it was a symbolic act of reclaiming self  
- **Secret:** He has crossed a threshold he didn’t notice — “I was bigger somehow. More there.” — a quiet transformation from survivor to force of will  
- **Secret belief:** The only thing that doesn’t lie is terrain — the ground under your feet, the stone, the physics of the world — a lesson from giants and Underdark  

## Uncertainty  
- **Dawnbringer’s gender:** Thorin says “she” in ch61, but in ch55 he says, “I don’t know what to call her... I don’t know what to call them.” The final attribution is inconsistent — later chapter says “she,” but the uncertainty lingers in the narrative voice.  
- **Current companions:** Last seen with Zalthir, Gyrgum, Daz, Glabbagool — but their current status as traveling with him is not confirmed after ch61.  
- **Exact location:** Last scene is in Deneir’s Sanctum preparing to use the reward to get a book — but whether he has left Candlekeep, and where he is now, is unspecified.  
- **Fate of the necromantic pearl and tooth:** Their current location (pocket?) is assumed, but no later confirmation of possession exists.  
- **Status of the Ember Vanguard:** The group is named and active, but whether it still functions as a unit after ch61 is unconfirmed.  
- **Conflict in allegiance:** He says “I want to be my faction,” but also seeks to join Eldeth Feldrun’s clan — are these contradictory, or has he reconciled them?  
- **Tremor Sense utility:** He explicitly regrets the feat, but no later feat substitution is mentioned — is it still active?  
- **Who is “Z” in “I dodged a bullet there with Z”?** Identity of “Z” is never clarified.  
- **“Sword whisperer” existence:** He believes a specialist exists — has he found one? No confirmation.

As a Bonus Action, you can take the Dash action. When you do, you gain 3 Temporary HP. You can use this trait 3 times, and you regain all uses after a Short or Long Rest.

### Reactions

**Gift of the Gem Dragon: Telekinetic Reprisal** • 3 / Long Rest
When you take damage from a creature within 10 ft. of you, you can use your reaction to force the creature to make a STR saving throw (DC 16). On a failure, the target takes 2d8 force damage and is pushed up to 10 ft. away from you. On a success, the target takes half as much damage and isn't pushed. You can use this reaction 3 times per long rest.
- Wisdom • The save DC for your Telekinetic Reprisal equals 16.
- Gift of the Gem Dragon: Telekinetic Reprisal: 3 / Long Rest • 1 Reaction

## Feats

**Ability Score Improvement** (PHB-2024 202)
Increase one ability score by 2 or two ability scores by 1.

**Gift of the Gem Dragon** (FToD 17)
When you take damage from a creature within 10 ft. of you, you can use your reaction to force the creature to make a STR saving throw (DC equals 11 + the ability modifier of the score increased by this feat). On a failure, the creature takes 2d8 force damage and is pushed up to 10 ft. away from you. On a success, the creature takes half as much damage and isn't pushed. You can use this reaction 3 times per long rest.
- Wisdom • The save DC for your Telekinetic Reprisal equals 16.
- Gift of the Gem Dragon: Telekinetic Reprisal: 3 / Long Rest • 1 Reaction

**Magic Initiate (Wizard)** (PHB-2024 201)
Two Cantrips. You learn two cantrips of your choice from the Wizard spell list. Int., Wis., or Cha. is your spellcasting ability for this feat's spells.

Level 1 Spell. Choose a level 1 spell from the Wizard spell list. You always have that spell prepared. You can cast it once without a spell slot, and you regain the ability to cast it in that way when you finish a Long Rest. You can also cast the spell using any spell slots you have.

Spell Change. Whenever you gain a new level, you can replace one of the spells you chose for this feat with a different spell of the same level from the chosen spell list.

Repeatable. You can take this feat more than once, but you must choose a different spell list each time.
- Wisdom •

**Sage Ability Score Improvements** (PHB-2024 183)
Increase two scores (+2 / +1) •

<!-- Backstory: gyrgum_backstory.md -->

Gyrgum’s backstory is defined not by the traditional, militaristic dogma of Bahamut, but by a radical, almost heretical theological interpretation of the Dragon God's will
. Born and raised within an orc tribe, Gyrgum learned early on how to navigate brutal hierarchies, noting that one could terrorize the orcs in his tribe simply by invoking the names of their superiors
. This harsh upbringing forged him into a highly practical creature, one who views survival in Underdark through a lens of pragmatism rather than blind zealotry
.
His path diverged significantly when he became a disciple of a mentor named Gorg’Bahamut (or Grog'Bahamut)
. Under this tutelage, Gyrgum studied esoteric texts like The Platinum Chronicle: Discourses on the Abyssal Blight and became fascinated by the historical events surrounding the Cult of the Dragon
. Gyrgum’s core philosophy is anchored in a legendary chess match between a dwarf named Stroud and the Gold Dragon Protanther, following the events at the Well of the Dragons
. While traditionalists believe Protanther intentionally threw the match to signal a divine alliance, Gyrgum’s sect believes Bahamut directly guided Stroud's hand, proving that individual agency and mortal choice can alter the cosmos
.
This historical event led Gyrgum to his most radical belief: dragons are defined by their freedom of choice, not the pigmentation of their scales
. To Gyrgum, chromatic dragons are not inherently evil by birth, and making judgments based entirely on a creature's race is the ultimate evil
. This worldview fuels his profound disgust for the bigotry he witnesses in Underdark, such as Eldeth Feldrun’s rigid hatred for all Duergar and orcs
.
Despite his scholarly interest in linguistics, the origins of names, and his habit of taking meticulous notes on deals and lore, Gyrgum expertly weaponizes his harmless exterior
. He readily plays the "Platinum Fool," using theatrical cooking performances to disarm and distract drow guards while his allies steal weapons
. Furthermore, he is not above employing deceptive theatrics with his magic, staging dramatic healing rituals complete with preacher-like shouts to manipulate the beliefs of those around him
. He views the chaos of Underdark as an interconnected web of transactional relationships, believing firmly that doing a good turn for someone will eventually yield a good turn in return
.

<!-- Ensemble dossier: npc_gyrgum.md -->

### Gyrgum

**Current Status:** Alive  
**Current Location:** Candlekeep, likely within Deneir’s Sanctum or the Flora Theca, engaged in scholarly investigation and theological discourse.  
**Allegiance/Faction:** Cleric of Bahamut; self-proclaimed founder of the Gyrgumite School of Theology. Formerly associated with the Ember Guard, the The Stone Guard, and The Keepers of the Flame — now operates as an independent investigator and theologian under Candlekeep’s authority.  

**Current Possessions / Notable Items:**  
- A magical short sword (claimed from the aftermath of the gelatinous cube battle).  
- A light crossbow (handed over without drama; confirmed well-maintained).  
- A bag of holding containing Glabbagool, the gelatinous cube companion.  
- A wax pouch retrieved from Asha Vandree Vandree’s body (suspected to contain theological text).  
- A brass key to the High Tower (received covertly from Kalan Strongbranch; now in Daz’s possession).  
- A collection of pamphlets on Bahamut’s lore (actively sought and collected).  
- A scrap of paper with meticulously recorded notes on all deals, events, and conversations.  
- Shrunken prayer beads (believed to be more potent in their reduced form).  
- A vial of Drow poison and poisoned hand crossbow bolts (acquired in ch03; likely still carried).  
- The *Fey-mous Flowers & Fungi* by Archibald Roots, Jr. (recently acquired and studied).  

**Notable Spells / Feats:**  
- Divine Healing (multiple uses, including instant restoration of Thorin).  
- Guiding Bolt (used with tactical precision; often paired with positioning).  
- Tasha’s Caustic Brew (cast in combat, though sometimes ineffective).  
- Inflict Wounds (used against The Pudding King).  
- Spirit Guardians (deployed to trap and damage enemies).  
- Revivify (carried and ready; used or available to restore fallen allies).  
- Lesser Restoration (known, but deliberately withheld to allow others to act mercifully).  
- Mending (rarely used but understood; critical in naval contexts).  
- True Strike (used in ritual combat).  
- Necrotic blast via holy symbol (used against cubes and oozes).  
- Acid expulsion (ritualized, self-inflicted channeling of acid as divine expression).  
- Glyph of Warding (designed and placed as a tactical trap).  
- Dust of Suleiman (prepared and deployed against the Heart Fungus).  
- Phantasmal Killer (prepared, though not cast).  

**Current Assignment / Role and Key Relationships:**  
- **Primary Role:** Investigator of the murder of Janussi at Candlekeep; theological scholar documenting the “Quadrangle Issue” and expanding Stroud’s Gambit into the Gyrgumite School.  
- **Key Relationships:**  
  - **Glabbagool:** His constant companion and arcane extension; he carries, feeds, and disciplines the cube, treating it as a sentient entity with emotional history.  
  - **Daz:** Trusted tactical partner; Gyrgum has handed him the High Tower key and provides healing and revival support.  
  - **Thorin:** Close ally and co-investigator; they share blunt pragmatism and mutual respect.  
  - **Zalthir:** Respected for combat prowess; Gyrgum acknowledges his skill and does not correct his theatrics.  
  - **Asha Vandree Vandree:** Former adversary, now a source of tactical insight; Gyrgum notes her with professional respect.  
  - **Bookwyrm (First Reader):** Authority figure at Candlekeep; Gyrgum suspects her generosity is conditional but respects her position.  
  - **Kalan Strongbranch:** Suspect in Janussi’s murder; Gyrgum understands his motive as professional humiliation.  
  - **Fheminor Scrivenbark:** Mysterious figure referenced in their own lying notes; Gyrgum suspects alignment with their goals.  

**Defining Recent Actions:**  
- Investigated the murder of Janussi, deducing the poison was delivered via a book laced with *midnight tears*, activated by saliva and triggered at midnight.  
- Recovered hidden writing on gift-wrapping paper using Bahamut’s restoration cantrip.  
- Published a theological reinterpretation of Stroud’s Gambit, introducing a “fourth corner” to the triangle of moral choices — triggering a mass debate among The Avowed.  
- Handled the High Tower key, recognizing its connection to the murder and sapphire summoning magic.  
- Delivered a burst of clean divine light to disable two helmed horrors during an ambush, confirming his role as primary support caster.  
- Refused to cast Lesser Restoration on Daral Yashenti, allowing the group’s collective mercy to unfold.  
- Diverted Bookwyrm (First Reader)’s tea into Glabbagool to avoid consumption while maintaining plausible deniability.  
- Documented the group’s moral compromise: choosing to expose embarrassment over murder as the central crime.  
- Identified that Bookwyrm (First Reader) is protecting family — an act he understands from personal experience (“I have done it for a mushroom”).  

**Revealed Motivations or Secrets:**  
- Gyrgum is deeply motivated by the pursuit of truth through documentation — he takes notes not for memory, but as sacred act.  
- He believes Bahamut does not reward spectatorship: “You see the weakness, now act.”  
- His theology centers on *choice* over determinism: dragons are not good or evil by scale color; moral alignment is forged by decisions.  
- He views Underdark’s corruption as a lesson in sacrifice: “Spend the cheap risk before the dear one.”  
- He is emotionally detached from trauma but deeply affected by moral compromise — he records it, does not mourn it.  
- He carries the weight of past failures: “Bahamut’s clerics never gave me a straight answer.”  
- His relationship with Glabbagool is not one of master and servant, but of mutual, silent understanding — he treats the cube as a companion with dignity.  
- He is spiritually adrift: “What does Bahamut actually want from me? Not the general want... but the specific want. The personal assignment.”  

## Uncertainty  
- **Contradictory Identity:** Multiple entries refer to “Gyrgum” — but all later evidence confirms the correct spelling is “Gyrgum,” and no separate entity exists.  
- **Current Companions:** While Glabbagool, Thorin, Zalthir, Daz, and Asha Vandree are last seen with Gyrgum, their current presence is not confirmed in ch61 — they may have departed or been separated during the helmed horror ambush.  
- **Glabbagool’s Status:** Gyrgum diverted tea into Glabbagool — implying the cube is still physically fused with him, but whether Glabbagool remains a distinct entity or has fully merged is unconfirmed.  
- **The “Second Faction”:** Gyrgum mutters “The Second Faction?” in ch23 — whether this is a named group, a placeholder, or an internal codename remains unresolved.  
- **Gyrgumite School’s Recognition:** While Gyrgum claims to be its founder and it is being debated, it is unclear whether Candlekeep’s authorities officially recognize it as a legitimate theological school.  
- **The Wax Pouch:** Gyrgum suspects it is the most important item from Asha Vandree’s body, but its contents are never revealed — only described as “tedious and boring.”  
- **The “Question of the Age”:** Gyrgum’s theological triangle is now a subject of debate, but the original question he was answering is never explicitly stated.  
- **Gyrgum’s Home:** He says he “just wanted to go home” in ch31 — but the location or nature of his home is never specified.  
- **The “Dawnbringer” Relationship:** Gyrgum says the bond between Daral Yashenti and Dawnbringer was “simple,” but the nature of Dawnbringer (person, weapon, entity?) remains ambiguous.  
- **The “Fheminor Scrivenbark” Entry:** The notes lie — the name is erased — but whether Fheminor Scrivenbark is alive, dead, a ghost, or a fabrication is unknown.  
- **The “Stroud’s Gambit” Origin:** Gyrgum claims to have coined the term, but whether Stroud himself was a real figure, a myth, or a fictional construct is never clarified.

<!-- Arc score: INTENTIONALLY TRACKLESS -->

Gyrgum has no formal arc score mechanic. This is a deliberate design choice — do not invent an arc score for this character and do not suggest creating one.

## Features & Traits

### Wizard Features

- **Core Wizard Traits (PHB-2024 165)**

- **Spellcasting (PHB-2024 165)**

- **Ritual Adept (PHB-2024 166)**

- **Arcane Recovery (PHB-2024 166):** When you finish a Short Rest, you can recover expended spell slots. The spell slots can have a combined level equal to no more than 4, and none of the slots can be level 6 or higher. You can use this feature once per Long Rest. 1/Long Rest • Special.

- **Scholar (PHB-2024 166):** You can choose one of the skills in which you're proficient and gain Expertise in it.

- **Wizard Subclass (PHB-2024 167):** Evoker

- **Evocation Savant (PHB-2024 174):** Choose two Evocation spells, each of which must not be higher than level 2, and add them to your spellbook for free. Whenever you gain access to a new level of spell slots in this class, you can add one Wizard spell from the Evocation school to your spellbook for free.

- **Potent Cantrip (PHB-2024 174):** When you cast a cantrip at a creature and you miss with the attack roll or the target succeeds on the saving throw, the target takes half the damage (if any) but suffers no additional effect.

- **Ability Score Improvement (PHB-2024 167)**

- **Memorize Spell (PHB-2024 167):** Whenever you finish a Short Rest, you can study your spellbook and replace one of the level 1+ Wizard spells with another level 1+ spell.

- **Sculpt Spells (PHB-2024 174):** When you cast an Evocation spell that affects other creatures that you can see, you can choose a number of them equal to 1 + the spell's level. The chosen creatures automatically succeed on their saving throws, and take no damage if they would normally take half on a successful save. Special.

- **8: Ability Score Improvement (PHB-2024 167)**

- **Keen Senses (PHB-2024 190):** You have proficiency in the Insight, Perception, or Survival skill.

- **Trance (PHB-2024 190):** You don't need to sleep, and magic can't put you to sleep. You can finish a Long Rest in 4 hours if you spend those hours in a trancelike meditation, during which you retain consciousness.

## Feats

- **Ability Score Improvement (PHB-2024 202):** Increase one ability score by 2 or two ability scores by 1.

- **Fey Touched (PHB-2024 204):** Ability Score Increase. Increase your Int., Wis., or Cha. by 1. Fey Magic. Choose one level 1 spell from the Divination or Enchantment school of magic. You always have that spell and the Misty Step spell prepared. Once per Long Rest, you can cast each of these spells without expending a spell slot. You can also cast these spells using spell slots you have of the appropriate level. The spells' spellcasting ability is the ability increased by this feat. Intelligence.

- **Magic Initiate (Wizard) (PHB-2024 201):** Two Cantrips. You learn two cantrips of your choice from the Wizard spell list. Int., Wis., or Cha. is your spellcasting ability for this feat's spells. Level 1 Spell. Choose a level 1 spell from the Wizard spell list. You always have that spell prepared. You can cast it once without a spell slot, and you regain the ability to cast it in that way when you finish a Long Rest. You can also cast the spell using any spell slots you have. Spell Change. Whenever you gain a new level, you can replace one of the spells you chose for this feat with a different spell of the same level from the chosen spell list. Repeatable. You can take this feat more than once, but you must choose a different spell list each time. Intelligence.

- **Sage Ability Score Improvements (PHB-2024 183):** Increase two scores (+2 / +1).

<!-- Backstory: daz_backstory.md -->

Daz presents himself to the Ember Vanguard as a pragmatic drow mercenary and wizard, operating as the tactical architect of their group.  Based strictly on what he has revealed and how he has acted, the players can infer that he is an exile or wanderer who spent his early life suffocating under the lethal machinations, rigid formalities, and totalitarian expectations of Menzoberranzan's noble houses and the Cult of Lolth .

His experiences within the Drow Orthodoxy have fostered a cynical detachment from the fanaticism of his peers, leading him to view the paranoid worldview of his homeland as a prison [Conversation History - 2026-03-23 21:26]. Uniquely for a drow, Daz has demonstrated a profound ideological rebellion against his culture's foundations by actively rejecting the institution of slavery. He has openly concluded that "free people are the basis of any society" and that relying on a slave caste inherently breeds insurmountable problems and political instability]. The party also knows he has spent time on the surface world, specifically navigating the High Forest, as he immediately recognized the falsehood of Prince Derendil's claims regarding a non-existent elven kingdom there.

Despite his ideological distancing from Menzoberranzan, Daz remains intimately familiar with the twisted, Machiavellian politics of his people. He frequently weaponizes this cultural knowledge to navigate the overlapping agentic flows of Underdark. The party has watched him expertly adopt a "Social Masquerade," playing the role of a haughty, domineering drow noble to manipulate enemies and command deference from local factions [. He leverages drow customs to secure tactical advantages, such as enforcing strict Non-Disclosure Agreements during negotiations and promising horrific supernatural consequences for breaches of secrecy]. Furthermore, he disdains inefficient power-sharing dynamics, accurately identifying them as "two in a box" politics—a temporary arrangement that merely masks weakness and inevitably invites assassination 

Methodologically, Daz is highly analytical, preferring to "overpromise and under-deliver" to control the expectations of those around him . To process the trauma of his past and sharpen his mental acuity, he obsessively documents the party's journey in a journal, an eccentric habit he adopted after reading the works of the surface-world author Milo Goodbarrel [ His calculated approach to survival and his ability to orchestrate complex political alliances have earned him the undisputed title of "General" within the Ember Vanguard

<!-- Ensemble dossier: npc_daz.md -->

name: Daz
type: npc

### Daz

**Current Status:** Alive.  
**Current Location:** On the path to Candlekeep, actively investigating the murder of Janussi and the mystery of his own origins.  
**Allegiance/Faction:** Independent; formerly associated with the Ember Vanguard, now operating as a pragmatic investigator with no formal allegiance. Has been the target of a high-value protective contract from House T'sarran and is suspected of being a living key to a hidden patron in Menzoberranzan.  

**Current Possessions / Notable Items:**  
- **Stonespeaker Crystal** — Used to communicate with beasts and interpret vibrational patterns.  
- **Dawnbringer** — Intelligent sunblade, carried and occasionally negotiated with.  
- **Collection of Duergar and Drow faction badges** — Used strategically for access and deception.  
- **Copy of Milo Goodbarrel’s book** — Referenced for psychological tactics, narrative theory, and historical parallels.  
- **Inert High Tower Key** — Invisible to magical detection; the true target of helmed horrors.  
- **Journal** — Kept daily since escaping Velkynvelve; records observations, faction dynamics, and personal reflections.  
- **Glabbagool** — Gelatinous ooze familiar, bound through performance and psychological manipulation.  

**Notable Spells / Feats / Abilities:**  
- **Evoker specialization** — Trained in Menzoberranzan; excels in precise, sculpted area-of-effect fire magic (Fireball, Scorching Ray).  
- **Fey Touched** — Grants two additional prepared spells and free castings; synergizes with the Princess Ebonmire spellbook.  
- **Warcaster** — Maintains concentration under duress.  
- **Misty Step** — Used for tactical repositioning and escape from grapples.  
- **Sculpt Spells** — Enables precise control over area effects to spare allies.  
- **Telekinetic** — Used as a geometry tool to reposition allies, not for offense.  
- **Shape Water** — Deployed for environmental manipulation, detection of hidden threats, and survival (e.g., freezing water into ice blocks).  
- **Magic Missile** — Primary offensive cantrip; used with clinical precision.  
- **Maximilian’s Earthen Grasp** — Used to immobilize or crush targets.  
- **Toll the Dead** — Employed against undead and resistant foes.  
- **Hypnotic Pattern** — Used to incapacitate enemies; requires concentration.  

**Current Assignment / Role and Key Relationships:**  
- **Lead Investigator** — Managing the murder investigation of Janussi at Candlekeep, piecing together connections between the stolen key, the sapphire summoning spell, the missing book fragment, and the Menzoberranzan-linked conspiracy.  
- **De Facto Tactical Leader** — Guides the group’s strategy with analytical precision, often framing decisions as group consensus while steering them toward his conclusions.  
- **Key Relationships:**  
  - **Zalthir** — Trusted tactical partner; shares awareness of environmental anomalies.  
  - **Gyrgum** — Lieutenant and ally; respects Daz’s judgment, confirmed his status as “the feared mage of the Ember Vanguard.”  
  - **Thorin** — Relies on Daz’s strategic insight; shares history with Dawnbringer.  
  - **Asha Vandree Vandree** — Believes Daz is a vessel of Lolth; his fabricated identity remains load-bearing.  
  - **House T’sarran** — Actively hunting him; believes he is bait for a hidden patron.  
  - **A’lai** — A drow with Underdark knowledge; Daz requires information from him.  
  - **The Unnamed Patron** — Someone in Menzoberranzan paid to protect Daz; identity unknown, but Daz suspects his value lies not in lineage, but in being a “living indicator.”  

**Defining Recent Actions:**  
- Successfully interrogated Sylvira Savikas, extracting her testimony implicating Kazryn Nyantani and Daral Yashenti, while recognizing her emotional truth and the Menzoberranzan-style framing of her as a target.  
- Identified the inert key in his pocket as the true target of helmed horrors, surviving their attack and casting Scorching Ray to defeat them.  
- Filed Daral Yashenti under “desperate,” recognizing his honesty born of impending ruin.  
- Concluded the Janussi murder investigation, documenting the confession and recognizing Kalan Strongbranch as the sole witness holding the complete record.  
- Rejected the notion of noble blood as an explanation for his value, preferring to believe his significance stems from something more complex — possibly tied to the erasure of the name “Daz’issin” from heraldic records.  
- Maintained his persona as a Lolth-follower with “consistent characterization,” not deception, to manipulate Asha Vandree’s perception.  
- Refused to cure Daral Yashenti, still weighing the decision.  
- Uses “118” as cryptic shorthand for a taster protocol, understood only by the group.  
- Continues journaling daily, analyzing every interaction as data, and treats information as finite, valuable currency.  

**Revealed Motivations / Secrets:**  
- **Motivation:** To understand his own identity and the reason powerful forces are invested in his survival. Rejects inherited status; seeks truth over narrative.  
- **Secret:** He is likely the last known bearer of the name “Daz’issin” — a diminutive form used by drow mothers for sons they hoped would survive. His name was deliberately erased from Menzoberranzan’s records, and his survival may be tied to a forbidden act or hidden lineage.  
- **Secret:** He has been the subject of a high-stakes, multi-faction game since his escape from Velkynvelve — not because he is powerful, but because he is a “bait” to reveal the identity of the patron who paid to protect him.  
- **Secret:** His persistent headache is not from sunlight, but from proximity to forbidden knowledge — possibly linked to the same corruption that drove Eldred and the Field Ward Preacher mad.  
- **Secret:** He is not merely a survivor — he is a living anomaly in a system designed to erase people like him. His honesty, a radical act in Menzoberranzan, is the key to unraveling the city’s hidden history.  

## Uncertainty  
- **Contradictory Origin Claims:** Multiple facts describe Daz as a Drow from Menzoberranzan, yet one fact (ch55) refers to him as a dwarf. Later facts consistently treat him as Drow and reference his drow heritage, so the “dwarf” reference is likely an error or misattribution.  
- **Identity of the Patron:** While Daz suspects someone in Menzoberranzan paid to protect him, the patron’s identity, motive, and affiliation remain entirely unknown.  
- **Nature of the “Struck” Record:** Daz suspects his name was erased from heraldic records, but the exact event, the responsible party, and the consequences are unconfirmed.  
- **True Nature of the Headache:** Is it caused by proximity to forbidden knowledge, the spreading madness of Underdark, or something else? No definitive cause is established.  
- **Status of Glabbagool:** Is the ooze familiar a product of manipulation, genuine bond, or something else? Daz treats it as a tool, but its sentience and loyalty are unconfirmed.  
- **Daz’s Name:** Is “Daz” a shortened form of “Daz’issin,” or is it a name he adopted? The connection is strongly implied but not confirmed.  
- **Daz’s Alignment with Lolth:** Is his Lolth-follower persona entirely fabricated, or has he developed genuine, if warped, belief? He claims it’s “consistent characterization,” but his internal monologues suggest ambiguity.  
- **Fate of the Patron’s Contract:** Is the contract still active? Has House T’sarran given up? Is A’lai connected to the patron? Unresolved.

<!-- Arc score: INTENTIONALLY TRACKLESS -->

Daz has no formal arc score mechanic. This is a deliberate design choice — do not invent an arc score for this character and do not suggest creating one.
